NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, May 13, 2014 
 
PRESENT: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jonathan 
M. Nez, Supervisor; and Jesse Thompson, Chairman were present in person. 
9:00 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Allen. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments. 
CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: April 22, 2014 b) Warrant and Voucher Report over $1000 April 2014 c) 
Clerk of Superior Court Report April 2014 d) Juvenile Probation Report April 2014 e) Constable Reports: 
Holbrook Precinct #1, March; Winslow Precinct #2, March; Show Low Prec inct #5, April; and Pinetop -
Lakeside Precinct #6, April 2014 f) Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, April; Winslow Precinct #2, 
April; Snowflake Precinct #3, April; Kayenta Precinct #4, April; Show Low Precinct #5, April; and Pinetop -
Lakeside Prec inct #6, April 2014 g) Tax exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42 -11153 as a Request for 
Redemption of Waiver h) Conduct board member elections for Woodruff Fire District, Clay Springs Domestic 
Water Improvement Districts and Northland Pioneer College on November 4, 2014 i) Adoption of the Proposed 
FY 2013 -14 Cost Allocation Schedule j) Contract amendment for Johnson Walzer & Associates for jail 
engineering support services and additional design for revised sallyport in the amount of $13,990.00 k) Contract 
amendment for Levi, Ray & Shoup, Inc. in the amount of $3,400 for additional services for website redesign 
project l) Department of Public Safety contract 2014 -048 for transfer of eight (8) handheld radars to the 
Sheriff’s Office m) Amendment number two to Intergovernmental Agreement #120067DC with the Department 
of Corrections for correctional officer certification training n) Letter to the Office of Navajo and Hopi Indian 
Relocation requesting contributions o) Sympathy letters to Honorable Ronnie Lupe an d Honorable Stanley 
Clitso p) Traffic control changes at the intersection of Burton Road and West Burton Road q) Resolution 
Number 10 -14, authorizing Navajo County to submit a grant application on behalf of the Hopi Education 
Endowment Fund, Hopi Tribe r) Sympathy letter to Robert Bohner : Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to 
authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Chairman Nez expressed his con dolences to  the 
Honorable Ronnie Lupe, Chairman of the White Mountain Apache T ribe for the loss of his wife and the 
Honorable Stanley Clitso, Kayneta Chapter President for the loss of his mother. 
      BOARD OF SUPERVISORS: Recognition of Kirk Grugel for h is recent community recognition as Citizen of 
the Year and Distinguished Victim Service Award : Brad Carlyon, County Attorney recognized Kirk Grugel for 
his recent community recognition as “Citizen of the Year” by the Pinetop-Lakeside Chamber of Commerce and 
also receiving  the “ Distinguished Victim Service Award ” at the Annual Navajo County Victim’s Rights 
Symposium on April 11th. Mr. Carlyon expressed his appreciation for the thousands of hours Mr. Grugel has put 
in and the hundreds of lives he has touched in making sure children are physically and mentally safe. Mr. 
Grugel addressed the Board regarding his  15 years of work for the children of Navajo County. He spoke about 
the need for awareness and education regarding  the abuse of children, to assist in repairing the problem s of 
abuse. Chairman Thompson  thanked Mr. Gru gel for his commitment in helping the lives of children. 
Supervisor Tenney  made comments about the adopted  children that his family has been involved with  and 
their lives being touched by Mr. Grugel’s work.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-13-2014 
    Page 2 of 4 
 
 
HUMAN RESOURCES:  
a. Presentation to retiring employee Dr. Wade Kartchner : Kimberly Eavenson recognized Dr. Wade 
Kartchner, Director of the Health Department  for his employment with Navajo County. Chairman 
Thompson expressed his thanks to Dr. Kartchner for his excellent work. Dr. Kartchner thanked all of 
those who he has worked with for the past nine years. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson reviewed the 17 item 
Personnel Action Report. Supervisor Te nney made a motion  to approve the personnel actions as 
presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of the donation of surplus old jail kitchen equipment to Navajo  
County Search and Rescue, INC : Mary Springer discussed the item  and answered questions of the 
Board. Supervisor Allen made a motion to approve the donation of surplus old jail kitchen equipment 
to Navajo County Search and Rescue, INC; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of the sale of a used fire hydrant to White Mountain Lakes Fire 
District in the amount of $1.00 : Mary Springer discussed the item. Supervisor Allen made a motion to 
approve the sale of a used fire hydrant to White Mountain Lakes Fire District in the amount of $1.00 ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
ADMINISTRATION: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 11-14, authorizing the sale 
of McHood Park to the City of Winslow : Homero Vela and Brandt Clark discussed the item. Mr. Clark 
explained that in 1988 the City of Win slow made an agreement with Navajo County for a 25 year lease that 
expired in 2013. The City of Winslow has an interest i n purchasing it at this time. Navajo County  would like to 
transfer ownership to be used as a community park , and if not used for that purpose, the park would revert back 
to Navajo County. Supervisor Nez made a motion to adopt Resolution Number 11-14, authorizing the sale of 
McHood Park to the City of Winslow ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. Mr. Jayne thanked Mr. Clark for his work on this project.  
PUBLIC WORKS : Consideration a nd possible approval of the expenditure of an amount not to exceed 
$135,000.00 from District I and $15,000.00 from District II Special Road Funds for the crushing and hauling of 
Red Dog Material : Homero Vela discussed the item. Supervisor Nez  made a motion  to approve the 
expenditure of an amount not to exceed $135,000.00 from District I and $15,000.00 from District II Special 
Road Funds for the crushing and hauling of Red Dog Material ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Allen; Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
      NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session : Supervisor 
Whitesinger made a motion to enter into the Navajo  County Public Health Services District  Board of 
Directors Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
a. CONSENT AGENDA: Intergovernmental Agreement #ADHS13 -036900, Amendment #2 with the 
Arizona Department of Health Services for the HIV Prevention Program, effective January 1, 2014 in 
the amount of $32,770.00: Mary Tyler discussed the consent item and answered questions of the Board . 
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion  to approve the Intergovernmental Agreement #ADHS13 -
036900, Amendment #2 with the Arizona Department of Health Services for the HIV Prevention

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-13-2014 
    Page 3 of 4 
 
Program, effective January 1, 2014 in the amount of $32,770.00 ; motion seconded by Supervisor Nez. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. At 9:42 a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to enter into Executive Session; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. EXECUTIVE SESSION pursuant to 
A.R.S. §38-431.03(A)(3) for the Board to receive legal advice from its attorneys regarding consideration 
of a Memorandum of Understanding #HU455044 with the Arizona Department of Health Services :  
Mary Tyler, Dr. Wade Kartchner and John Zimmerman were present for discussion of the item. 
c. At 9:56 a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to reconvene into regular session; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Nez . Vote unanimous approving the motion.  RECONVENE for possible approval of a 
Memorandum of Understanding #HU455044 with the Arizona Department of Health Services ; 
Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the  Memorandum of Understanding #HU455044 with 
the Arizona Department of Health Services ; motion sec onded by  Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
            Supervisor Nez made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen . Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.   
      COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Nez  congratulated a ll the graduates in obtaining higher learning . He wished t he mothers, 
grandmothers and all  a Happy Mother’s Day. He indicated t hat many Memorial Day  celebrations are  
forthcoming and expressed appreciation to all of the veterans. Supervisor Nez  indicated the Navajo Nation 
Council leadership has changed temporarily. Supervisor Nez reported he is now the Vice -Chair of the Navajo 
Nation budget committee. He invited all to the Winslow Indian Health Care Center Conference on June 17th and 
18th at Twin Arrows. Supervisor Nez  indicated he will be a speaker at the event  to discuss living a healthy 
lifestyle. Supervisor Allen reported she met with James Jayne and Supervisor Tenney regarding the budget. 
She indicated she was a speaker at the Snowflake -Taylor Chamber of Commerce meeting regarding  natural 
resource issues, Mexican wolf expansion, and endangered species.  Supervisor Allen stated she attend ed the 
White Mountain Apache Tri be Inauguration Ceremony at Whiteriver. She indicated that she was appointed by 
the Arizona Corporation Commission to the Transmission Line Siting Committee . She stated she attended the 
first public hearing regarding the authorization of a substation and three transmission lines. Supervisor Allen 
indicated the WIA had their board meeting where they discussed a program set up through NPC to help about 
20 people to get their GED’s  and various credits. She reported the AZ/NM Coalition  of Counties is  having a 
Celebration on July 19 th at the Eager Fairgrounds to celebrate their 25 year anniversary. She stated she enjoyed 
the 100 Club  event. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Supervisor Allen  and Chairman Thompson  for 
accompanying her to the White Mountain Apache Tribe Inauguration Ceremony for the Tribal Chairman, 
Ronnie Lupe, Vice -Chairman, Kasey Velasquez and the Tribal Council Elect.  She indicated Chairman Lupe 
was sworn in for a 9 th term. She indicated she attended the Arizona Town Hall in Tucson regarding Ari zona’s 
vulnerable populations which include s any family who may be struggling.  Supervisor Whitesinger reported 
she attended the White Mountain SAFE House Board meeting  where they discussed a fundraiser for those who 
are in need of the services offered by t he SAFE House. Supervisor Whitesinger reported the White Mountain  
Youth Corp will be helping with the new walking trail at the Holbrook complex and the ground breaking will be 
on June 7 th. Supervisor Tenney  indicated he met with Senator Flake  and Senator H einrich in Sho w Low 
regarding forest issues the forest industry is facing in regards to forest treatments . Supervisor Tenney reported 
on his t rip to Portland Oregon where he attended  the National Forest Symposium  on PILT  payments. He 
indicated he will be attending a WIR conference where they will also discuss PILT  on the federal level . He 
indicated he attended a 4FRI meeting with the Governor’s staff and some of the principals of 4FRI. Supervisor 
Tenney stated he and Hunter Moore met with Cal Joiner, the R egional Forester and some of the staff from the  
Washington office. He indicated they held  discussions on  our forest industry. James Jayne, County Manager

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-13-2014 
    Page 4 of 4 
 
spoke of the community clean ups and the importance of this partnership with the communities . Brad Carlyon, 
County Attorney reported that  the Navajo County Public Safety Fundraiser fo r the 100 Club of Arizona was 
held on Saturday, May 3rd in Lowe’s parking lot in Show Low. Mr. Carlyon indicated that  the fundraising raffle 
will continue through June  8th when they will have the drawing. He indicated t he Navajo County Attorney’s 
Family Advocacy Center had their grand opening of their new Holbrook facility on May 8th. Mr. Carlyon stated 
the building was dedicated to  Dr. Hannah Rishel, who was instrumental  in getting a family advocacy center. 
Mr. Carlyon indicated he, Supervisor Tenney and Sheriff Clark will be putting flyers on pizza boxes to remind 
graduates to be responsible on graduation night and to celebrate in a responsible manner. Mr. Carlyon indicated 
this coming Saturday  his wife , Anna , Dave Roberts  and others  will participate in a 30 mile walk , which is 
raising donations  for cancer. Chairman Thompson  thanked Mr. Vela  for working with ADOT  to acquire 
milling material to be used for District II roads . He reported he attended the White Mountain Apache Tribe 
Inauguration. Chairman Thompson indicated he was a speaker at the Dilcon Community School ’s 8 th grade 
student’s graduation. He indicated he focused  his speech on the importance of education, to dream bi g and to 
work hard . Chairman Thompson  reported he, Brad Carlyon and Sheriff Clark attended the retirement 
reception in Winslow for Dale K. Patton, Winslow City Attorney. He indicated he attended faith based 
organizations meeting where they are trying to get C ongress to understand the religiou s freedoms among the 
Native Americans. Chairman Thompson also spoke of water shortages  and the need of more  discussions for 
water resources on the reservation. Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the B oard indicated that professional p hotos will 
be taken in the Code Talkers room following the Board of Supervisors meeting. 
      ADJOURN: At 10:24 a.m. Supervisor Allen  made a motion to adjourn ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
DATE:  May 27, 2014 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
